INTRODUCTION 


The purpose of living is to discover the purpose of living. 
Our ancestors taught us that Astrology was one of the keys to 
the solution of this enigma. The importance of Astrology was 
well stressed by Vaidyanatba Dikshita when he said in bis treatise 
‘Jatakaparijata” that there is nothing in the world ilke a horos- 
cope to help a man in the acquisition of wealth, ta save him like 
a boat Ina sea of tronbles and to serve him as a guide in his 
journey, The science of Astrology was beld in such esteem in 
ancient Tadia as to be recognized aw one of the Angas of the 
Veda, though the term-‘JyothIsha Sastra’ as it was then known 
included both Astronomy and Astrology. As Dr. Ralph Waldo 
Eqereon wrote, “Astrology is Astronomy brought to earth and 
applied to the affairs of men.’ Of what use is Astronomy to 
mankind unless it is interpreted in terms of our dafly life and 
thought? Even well-known Astronomical authoritles have 
admitted that the manner in which the castern nations bad set 
forth to make elaborate and costly arrangements for continual 
observation of celestial phenomena was not Intended to advance 
the cause of astronomy but to gain results of great asteological 
value, They built observatories in order that, in the long run, @ 
trustworthy system of astrology could be deduced by them ftom 
the study of the motions of the heavenly bodies. (nh our own 
country, the subject was studied as part of the cusriculum in the 
‘guru kulas’ though in recent times its study has been neglected, 


Time is of the essence in Astrology as much asin any other 
aspect of life, Man cannot fulfil bis obligations in society with- 
out devising ways of recording the flow of time correctly. Phy- 
cists aré aware of the importance of time measurement to them, 
An apricolturist should know the correct time of sowing and 
harvesting. Religious festivals have to ne chservcdio time. And 
so the need for correct astronomical ephemeris of panchangas ts 
abvious, 


A panchang or almanac!s« book of informaticn relating tothe 
Jongitudes, etc. of the Sun, the Moon and the planets together with 
the related phenomena. Tt contains astronomical calculations 
and fornishes the thithis (the lunar day), Vara (the week day). 
Nakshatra (the position of the Moon in its orbit}, Yoga and 
Karana. A panchang js to an Astrologer as finance is to a 
busineseman. 


At present, & large number of almanacs appear in India every 
year from different pacts of the country and in different languages. 
The makers of these panchangs follow the siddhanta that appeals 
to them, though Surya Siddhanta fs the one widely followed. 
During the period from 400 AD to 1200 AD, knowa as tbe period 
of Siddbanta Fyothisha, sa many as cighteen siddbantas were in 
application and these siddhantas persist ia varying degrees in our 
panchangs even to this day. No wonder, therefore, these 
almanacs vary widely in the positions of the planets and the time 
when a thithi, nakehatrn, yoga and karana beginsorends. Even 
here, the thme is stated in terms of ghatis and vighatis whioh were 
employed when watches and clocks were uot available. Would 
it not be advisable now to changs over Co the time measuremen? 
{o hours and minutes which is in universal use rather thao con- 
fure the reader by compelling nim to find out the time of Sutyo- 
haya jn the locality, convert the ghatis aod vighatis into equl- 
valent hours and minutes and then add it to the Suryodhayakala 
to arrive at the moment of beginning or end, as the case may be, 
ofa thithi or nakshatra. No two almanacs agtce as to when the 
aun coters a particular rasi and when it leaves it and enters the 
next rasi or when the New Moon, Full Moon or Amavasya 
(Moon-Sun conjunction) occurs. While one panchang considers 
8 certain time of a day beneficial for commencing a certain under- 
taking belong ruled by an auspicious yoga-according to jt, the 
other panchangs differ. There is utter confusion in the pan- 
changa io such fun‘tamental matters like the beginniag of the 
yess, the solar months and day, the lunar month and thithls. 
There does not seem to be any uniformily among the panchang 
makers even in regard to the dates when the religious festivals 
aro to be celebrated. The seasons have lost their traditional 
association with the months and very frequently festivals ag given 
io the panchangs fall in the wrong season. The consultant ia 


xi 


naturally confounded and ls af a loss to know which panchang to 
follow. Can not all almanacs ensure uniformity and follow the 
Jead given by the National Caleodar and prepare their panchangs 
makiog use of the accurate dala computed by the Nautical 
Almanac Unit of the Government of India in accordance with the 
most up-to-date astronomical formulae and arrive at the 
Nirayana positions of the nlanets by subtracting the Ayanamsa 
commonly fa use? Such a step, | am sure, will go far in narro- 
wing the wide gap now existing among the almanacs. 


There exist a set of people who are blind to reason and still 
follow and advocate the vakya system of calculations, The vakya 
system came into being when the calculations suggested by Arya- 
bhata to conform to the changes that had crept in the planetary 
mations since the time of Surva Siddbanta were found to bs 
lengthy. To avoid this, certain astronomers Jike Varvrachi 
invented the Vakya method whereby readymade, calculated long- 
itudes for small cycles and great cycles were worked out and it 
was claimed that with the help of vakyas the planetary positions 
including the Moon can be calculated with the Facility. 


Can we imagine two sets of heavenly bodies, one answering 
to the Followers of vakya calculations and the other to those who 
adopt the ‘Drik’ system? Impossible. It appears that the makers 
of vakya panchanga due to their ignorance or dislaclination to 
learn the present methods of calculation are simply claiming 
superiority on antiquity and followlag. 


We are all sifted with the facutty of reason and discrimi- 
natton and, if as a result of teat, we come to the conclusion that 
the ‘Drik’ method alone is correct we should let our reason 
prevail to the exclusion of all other factors. A wise man Is 
compared to the swan which knows how to separate milk combined 
wlth water, Quar ancicats did expect us to revise the calculation 
from time to thme so thet the results arrived at agree with che 
scientific and occular vetification. According to them “Drik 
karanakya vihvenah, Kbetas sthoolahas karmanam arbah.” That 
is to say, the positions of the planets found out by using a 
calculation incousist with observational precession will only be 
approximate and rough and oot fit for religiouy observances. 


zi 


They have also advised us to observe religious functions a. a 
time arrived at according fo ‘Drik’ calculation alone “‘Mirtahem 
Samerikramya Chandalah Koti Janmasn’. He who misses the 
day of anniversary of departed sovl is sure to be penalised by 
births a crore of times. That they do not want us to commit any 
error in this respect, is thus amply clear, 


“Drik” means ‘to see’. There is no such distinction in 
astronomical calculations as visible calculations like the eclipses 
and invisible calculations like the thithis for which inaccurate 
vakya -calculations could be followed as advocated by the 
followers of the Vakya system. Thithis ate also visible as they 
are 12 degree distances between che Sun and the Moon and can 
be measored by astronomical instruments, and the same Sun and 
Moon which cause eclipses cause thithis also. The simplest 
method to verify the accuracy or otherwise of any system is to 
observe the moment when an eclipse occurs or the moment when 
it is ‘New Moon or Full Moon and see whether it coincides with 
the moment arrived at according to that system. The Drik system 
passes the test, while the Vakya system utterly falls, If the time 
of eclipse according to the vakya calculation is wrong, on the 
game analogy the beginning and ending moments of the thithis 
calculated according to that system should also be wrong. Are 
we still right in observing anniversaries of departed souls and 
performing other religious functions according to the inaccurate 
vakya thithis ? 


There is difference between the calculations, old and new: for, 
the elements of the planets and the data employed in the old 
methods have not been subjected to any change, while in the new 
method these have been rectified from time to time. While con- 
verting the mean position of the planet (madhya graha) to the 
true position (sputa graha), the old method employed only a small 
number of inequahtics while the new system takes several 
inequalities iato account. In respect of the Moon, the maximum 
difference between the Vakya aud Drik sysiems may work out 
to 17 ghatikas of time (6 hours, 43 minutes) in thithi! and 
nakshatra which are two important angas of the almanac, Sa 
alao the position of Mars and Mercury give vast disparity in the 
new system from those obtained according to the old method. 


xt 


The Hindus fix the dates of festivals, anniversary, birth day 
and other ceremonies only on an astronomical! basis. They do 
not observe the rule “Thirty days have September, April, June 
and November.” The birth days or the anniversarles are 
celebrated not on fixed dates of fixed months, but based upon 
thithis and nakshatras. As already said before, no religious 
practices should be observed at wrong moments. Even for 
Astrology, the calculations must be accurate, In Uttara 
Kalamrita, Kalidasa makes it very clear in Sloka 4 of Kanda J 
that the exact positions of the planets are to be calculated 
according to the Drik method. Mantreswara echoes the same 
sentient when he says in Phala Deepika “Ghathir Drik 
Tbhulyathvam Ganitha Karanaithi’, that is the position of the 
planets coincident with observation should be used. We will do 
well to follow the Drik system for then we shall be wise, others 
otherwise, 


When Westeners publish Ephemeris worked out by great 
astronomers of today who calculate correctly to land in moon at 
a specified point at the schedule moment, I do not find why we 
should separately work and briag out almanuace to erect dozen 
horoscopes to the same person. Simply use that Ephemeris where 
daily position of planets is given; this book is a self-iostructor. 
Let it serve all and let us sce that one person has only one 
horoscope.

THE MOTHER EARTH 


The earth, which is our home, offers a deceptive appea- 
rance, as though it is flat, plain and circular, with its boundary, 
the horizon, But It is actually rouad, like a ball. 


Gallleo used the interesting and simple method to prove that 
the earth is round like a sphere. He observed the face of the 
earth during lunar eclipse aad noted the shadow of the earth on 
the moon. Lt was, as itis seen evea now, distinctly circular. The 
eclipse of the moon may jast for some hours. Yet duc to the 
spinning of the earth its circular shadow on the moon remains, 
during the duration of eclipse, it does not get changed. 


Anotber proof is that, if a person commences his journey 
from one place—say, Madras—aad moyea In the same direction, 
either due Bast or due North or due South without changing 
his course, be will retura to his starting place, i.c., Madras itself, 
It will be just like an ant, proceeding in any one direction on 
an apple or on a lime fruit, returatng to the same starting 
point. 


There is also a direct proof, after so much advancement of 
the science. ff one looks at the earth from a far-off place, in 
space away from the earth, one will find that the earth appears 
like a ball. We, oo the earth, cannot note it. But photograph 
taken from a great distance by the Rockets which are fired up 
will show the curvature of our earth quite clearly and distinctly.

No one had ever pone around the earth with a measuring tape or

Ecatosthenes took the angular elevation height at which the Sua 
appeared at the same time from two cities situated at @ fairly 
long distance, say 500 miles. He found the difference in the 
angle of vision. The World is round. i.e., 360°. So he cuicula- 
ted the distance between these two cities and took the difference 
in the anples for calculation. He divided 360° by this difference 
in the angles of vision and multiplied the result by the known 
distance between these two cities. Similar calculaiions were 
made on different occasions and all the results were found *o be 
the same. Thus the circumference is worked out. Then the 
diameter 13 calculated as it is impossible to measure if. Taoere 
is the difinite mathematical relationship between the diameter 
of a circle and its circumference 22/7. So the diameter is also 
arrived at by using the formula tbat circumference whea multi- 
plied by sevea and the product divided by twenty-two gives the 
diameter,

Technically speaking, it is the projection of the earth’s Equator, 
upon the celestial sphere. The Celestial Equator divides the

The measurement is always made for astronomical purposes 
along and across the Celestial Equator; this is called the Right 
Ascension and Declination respectively. 


But astrologers use another method of fising up Planeta by 
measuring along the apparent Sun's anoual path which is called 
Ecliptic, The sun appears to move strictly in tbe Ecliptic. It is 
more or less a circle; it cuts the Celestial Equator in two points, 
diametrically opposite ton cach other. The Ecliptic is inclined by 
23° 27' to the Celestial Equator due to the inclination of the axis 
of the earth. If one observes the movements of the Planets, the 
Moon, Jupiter etc., one will find that they also move io their 
individual orbits which are stightly inclined to the Ecliptic. 


Abyhow no planet can proceed either north or south of the 
ecliptic by morc than 5", Hence, if a parallel line on either side 
of the ecliptic is drawn at a distance of 74°, then the ecliptic will 
te in the middle whereas on either side there will be a broad 
pathway. If both the North and the South of the ecliptic are 
considered as a broad pathway with 15° declination, then this 
pathway, io which all planets can be located at any time, is 
called the Zadiac. 


It may be cefined as the apparent pathway of all the planets 
belonging ta Solar System extending to 74° N, and 74° S, of the 
ecliptic. 


Measurement |s always commenced from Vernal Equinox. 


Therefore, for the use of astrologers to fix the positions of 
tae Planets, the Ecliptic is chosen as the circle of reference, The 
distance measured in one direction along the Ecliptic from the 
Vernal Equinox Is called Celestial Longitude and the distance 
measured perpendicular to the Ecliptic is termed as the Celestial 
Latitude, 


Though the tracks of the Planets are all found to lie very 
nearly along the line, they will be only a few degrees North or 
South of it, at the most 74° to 8°. The Celestial Latitude is 
absolutely different from Declination except et the two equinoc- 
tial points, This measurement is convenient for astrological 
purposes aud is also necessary for predictions.

In short, Celestial Latitude is the perpendicular distance 
between a Planct and the Ecliptic (whatever be the declination of 
he Ecliptle itself) whereas declination is the perpendicular dis- 
tance counted from the Celestial Equator to the planet or star. 
Celestia! Longitude is measured along the Ecliptic from the 
Vernal Equinox whereas the Right Ascension is measured along 
the Celestia! Equator from the Vernai Equinox in one direction, 


If two objects are in equal declination either on the same 
side of the Celestial Equator ot on oppoalte side of it, they are 
said to be Parallel to each other.

Sidereal time is nearly but not exactly atar time. 7¢ is vernal

about 2Zist March. The sidereal clock shows 24 hours when the 
earth has rotated once; but it registers 4 more minutes (roughly) 
when the ordinary clock shows 24 hours. {fn 2 months, the 
sidereal time advances by 4 hours. In 6 months it gains |? houra 
and in one year it gains exactly 2 hours, i.c., ope day.

ofangle. Hence if one ls Known, the other can be calculated,

STANDARD TiMe: For each country, a Standard Time 
Geographical meridian is chosen, Clocks are adjusted accord- 
ingly, to keep uniform time throughout the country for conveni- 
ence in matters pertaining to Railways, Telegraphs, Phones, 
Radic and astronomical calculations. In Great Britain, the 
Standard time is Greenwich Meantime. JT is observed over the 
whole of the country instead of the true focal time without 
taking into consideration haw for East or West of Greenwich 
a place may be.

For India, the meridian of 82° 30° Bast of Greenwich was 
arbitrarily chosen in 1606 and hence the Standard Time for Indla 
is 5} hours in advance of Greenwich Mean Time. At all places 
situated on this longitude of 82° 30’ the Sun will be at noon at 
the Zenith, In these places, the Sun will be in meridian nearly 
5% hrs. early than at Greenwich situated Weat of India. 
(nearly ;— variations will occur a little, by difference In the 
Longitudes of the places). People in Japan, and East Chinz 
observe the Sun rising earler thas io India as they are to the 
East of India. In Arabia, time fs later than in indfa, and artill 
later to Paris, London etc. In America which is situated far 
West of Greenwich, the Sun rises much later than at Greenwich. 
Local Mean Time 


The earth moves round in an eclipse and revolves about 
itself. Hence the time of Sunrise varies from place to place jn 


24 


different longitudes. The earth revolves 60° in one day. One 
day is divided Into 24 hours, or 1,440 minutes. So to revolve 1° 
it takes 4 minutes. Hence,‘places in the same latitude about 1° 
Weat of any one place will see the Sun rising 4 minutes later 
than at the eastern place. 1I/two places A and B are 14° apart 
and A {s east of B, B, will note the Sun just rising ene hour after 
sunrise at A. Hence the Local Mean time is one which can be 
arrived at, after calculating the Longitude of the place. ft fs 
customary to calculate it, by taking Greenwich as the place of 
reference and fixing the longitude of the place. Conversion of 
Standard time to L:-cal Mean time and calculation of the Sidereal 
time at any particular moment are to be clearly understood by 
the students of astrology.

THE EPHEMERIS 


The best available and the most popular ephemeris is 
‘Raphael's astronomical Ephemerls’ for every year commencing 
from 1st January and ending on 31st December, 


Various data regarding the date, the day, the sidereal time at 
noon at Greenwich, Sun’s Lonpitude and declination, Moon’s 
Longitude, Latitude and Declination and midnight position of 
Moon are given on cne page. tn the adjacent page, Longitude 
of Neptune, Uranus, Satura, Jupiter, Mars, Venus and Mercury 
will be found. These particulars are published in the lower 
half of cach page. The data in the upper half of these pages 
may be taken for consideration later. 


Data fer the month of January are given on pases 2&3; for 
February on pages 4&5; for March on pages 6&7 and so on: 


Tn the first column in pages 2, 4, 6, 8 and 10 etc., the date of 
the month is givenin order. To guide one properly and to avoid 
committing any mistake. while referring slonz one tine, for every 
§ dates, a rule is drawn from the beginning of the left page ta the 
end of the right one. 


Io the second column, D/W will be seem Ft means the day 
of the week. For Sunday, a flowery canital ‘S’ is used, capital ‘3’ 
For Saturday. ‘Th’ for Thursday, ‘Tu’ fur Tuesday 'M’ for 
Monday, ‘“W’ for Wednesday and ‘F’ for Friday, are the abbre- 
viations used. 


The third cotumn is allotted to the Sidereal time. ‘H’ shows 
‘Hours’ ‘M’ represents minutes and ‘S’ indicates seconds. 


Io the fourth column, a black thick and dark dot in the 
centre of a circle is seen. It is the symbol of Sun. ‘Long’ stands 
for Longitude. Underneath the Longitude of Sun is given its 
position in degrees. Neat you will aote the syaivo! of the sign 
in which Sun is on that duy. Minutes and seconds follow tbe 
symbol of the sign occupied by Sun. 


The next column is allotted to the declination of Sun. This 
shows bow far Sun is in the North of Scutn of C-lestiad Equator. 
On or around June 22nd, Sun will be in 23° 27° North; op or 


32 


around September 22nd, Sun’s declination $s 0°; J.c., Sun is 
exactly In the Celestial Equator: j.¢., Sun passes the autumnal 
equinox, Gradually, Sun’s declination increases. I[t will be 
found In the South of the Celestial Equator. On of around 22nd 
December, the declination of Sun will be 23° 27’ South. Sun 
takes a northern course aod arcund March 21st, the declination 
of Sun fs 0", It indicates that Sun on that day passes the Vernal 
Equinox, So, if one simply follows this columo, day after day, 
and month after month, one can understand the declinatloa of 
Sun. 


There is no latitude for Sun. Why? Thy Sun’s apparent 
path is the ecliptic. The latitude is the distance measured from 
the ccliptic perpendicularly to the planet. As Sun moves 
along the ecliptic and the measurement is to be taken only 
from the ecliptic, there is no latitude at all for Sun. It may 
be sald that Latitude of Sun is ever Zero. But, all other 
planets have their own orbits. They are not parallel to the 
Sun's path—the ecliptic, But they are inclined to it. So, each 
of them crossea the ecliptic im two different poiuts. 


If one goes through the ephemeris, one can observe that 
the planets have different latitudes and declinations. 


The sixth column given the longitude of Moon. The scventh 
shows ita latitude, whereas the 8th is the declination of Moon, 


Since Moon moves very fast and there is variation in tts 
Motion ¢very day, the 9th and the 10th columns in the same 
page show the Moon’s longitude and latitude at midnight 6 
hours. Thus for Moon alone, its position for every 12 hours 
can be had from the ephemeris. 


Ip the adjacent page, date of month is givea and only on Sun- 
days, instead of giving the dates, the letter ‘S’ wili be seen 
which, we will realise to be useful. 


The longitudes of planets are given in a particular order. 
The planet which moves very very slowly is Neptune, So 
its longitude is given first. No dowbt Uranus also moves 
slowly, yet faster than Neptune and it is between Neptune and 
Sun. So longitude of Uranus is given next to that of Neptune 


33 


The tourth colursn is for Saturna, It is nearer to Sun than 
Uranus. Foltowing the same principle longitudes of Jupiter, 
Mars, Venus and Mercory ate given ja the ephemeris in the 
order of their distances from Sua. 


Next refer to the apper half of the pzge. On the right 
side, i.e, pages 3, 5,7, 9, and so ov, the &th column gives the 
position of Moon’s node, which Hindus call as Rahu. As the 
position of Kethu (Descending node) is always exactly 18° away 
from Rabu, it is not given separately. 


Thus, the date, tue day, the sidereal time al noon 41 Green- 
wich, the longitudes of all plinets can be had from the 
ephemeris.

SIGNS AND HOUSES 


No it is necessary to know what a sign is and what a house 
means, 


Thousands of stars are visible when night gathers und they 
are scattered at fandom over the sky. Only a few appear in 
certain patterus, [Uf ane observes the pathway of the moon, one 
will notice thet it does not embrace all the stars ia the heavens» 
but takes a course whtch is in the neighbourhoad of the celestial 
equator. Observations made to Gx the apparent pathway of the 
Sun both in the early morning hours and immediately after sun- 
set confirm that its route is also very close to the celestial 
equator and neither Sun nor Moon moves far away from it, 
Never do they pass near the South Pole or the North Pole. The 
Sun’s path crosses the celestial equator io two points and the Sun 
will be away from it 24° 27’ at the most. The Moon's orbit is a 
Uttle inclined to the Sun's path, viz the ecliptic. The other planets 
also have their individual orbits and a!lof them are found with- 
in & degrees on either side of the Ecliptic. This belt of the 
heavens, which is a circle of space surrounding the Barth, is 
called the Zoptac, 


The Sua appears to rise 345 times a year. But 350 is the 
LCM, (Least Common Multiple) of Numbers 1 to iQ except 7. 
Hence it appears that the circular route is said to cover 360 
degrees, and the mathematicians have also taken 36U" for a 
circle. 


The Sun and the New Moon conjom twelve times before the 
Sun completes one revolution. So also in the same one- year 
period there are [2 Full Moons. 


Probably this may ke the reason to divide the zoptac into [2 
equat perts. Hence each part is defined to contain 30 degre?s 
of epace called the signs of the Zodiac. Thus the 12 signs, erch 


41 


measuring exactly 30 degrees, constitute the circle of the Zadiac 
which is 360°. 

These twelve signs commence from the vernal equriox and 
they are called, Aries, Taurus etc. to Pisces. For convenience the 
various names and symbols of the signs and the planets are given 


below.

The earth revalves on its axis once in 4 hours. During the 
period the twelve signs pass over any given spot on the earth, 
once succeeding the other in the same order. Each sign takes 
nearly two hours to transit the eastern part of the horizon where 
the Sun appears fo rise in the morning. The time taken by each 
sign varies. Though a few transit within a period of two hours, 
some signs take more than two hours and the total time taken is 
only 24 hours. This aiso varies widely, depending upon the latitude 
of the place of birth. 

For example, Aries takes nearly 112 minutes and Gemini 129 
minutes for people in the Equator, whereas Aries takes 80 minutes 
and Gemini 118 minutes for peopie in the latitude 34° North. 


ARBAB BSS aos 


42 


As the Zodiac is ever moving aad bitth casa happen at any 
moment, the part of the Zodiac that rises in the East will be 
regularly changing at the rate of roughly 1 degree for every four 
minutes. Therefore, to find out which part of the Zodiac or 
which part of a particular sign, is clsing in the East at the time 
of birth one bas ta calculate. [tis only to ascertain this exact 
position and to have some more data, sidercal time at lhe time of 
birth of the child is worked out. Now it is necessary to know 
how to fix the ascendant, ie, the part of the sign that is rising 
in the East, 


With a view to read the results of the horoscope our sages 
have also advocated that the circle commencing from the 
uscendant is to be divided into 12 parts. These divisions are 
called Houses. The rising position in the Zodiac is called the 
Ascendant. The meridlan of the place and the setling position 
of the zodiac, otherwise called Desceodaot, are (aken for 
calculation, ‘She quodranots formed between the meridian and 
the horizon ure trisected, {hese from the visible nix houses 
ubove the carth. The other six houses and diametrically 
oypasite to cach of these six houses. Thus there are six houses 
below the earth. 


There are various methods of House division. The Hindus 
follow the method recommended by Sripatipadhdhati. I¢ is 
popular. [a other couatries, cight methods are followedland the 
prroviples in atved in cach wal be very briefly mentioned here. 


(a) Ptolemy's modus equalis or equal method. The division 
of the houses commences from the degree that rises in the East. 
Thirty degrees are added successively and the meridian obtained 
will vot coincide with the zenith, It is a rough aicthod and is 
given up. 


(bd) Porphry suggested a method where-the meridian and 
the ascendant are decided and the quadrants ure equally divided 
inty three equal parts The quadrant formed by the ascendant 
aad Nadw (tue cusp of the fourth bousc) ic divided into three 
equal parts. Thus, six hous s are obtained and the other six 
Houses, the diametrically oppusite poiots are taken. ma is also 
wul used oowadays,

(c) Morinuy jotroduced a method by trisecting the 
quadrant of Ube equator by circles passing through the poles of 
the ecliptic which has not received support from the present-day 
authors, 


(d} Ao Australian astrologer named Zarie) advocated equal 
division method dividing the equator into 12 equal segments. 
The division commences from the meridians of the place. This is 
also not popular, 


(¢) The method of Alcabitius: He determines the Ascen- 
ant In the usual manner. He determines the sidereal time when 
this degree of the ascendant will be in the meredian. The 
difference between this and the siderea! time at birth is divided 
by three and that position'which will culminate for the sidereal 
time thus arrived at, is taken as the cusp of houses Il and 12. 
Sicllarly he completes the circle. 


(f) Campanous follows a method by which he finds the cusps 
of houses which are the degrees of the Ectiptic cut oy the Prime 
Vertical. 


1B} The method in commen use is that followed by Placidus, 
geatfally knowns as “Semi Are System'’, 


(4) Regiomontanus advocates modus rationalis which is a 
rationa! cae,

In WestekRN Countries in the circular one they erect the 
chart and the planets are inserted according to the Houses they 
occupy. Thigis “Savana System.” 


(4) Westerners do not draw the horoscope, sign after sigo. 
But the twelve liaes which appear like the spokes in a wheel are 
the 12 cusps which are the partitions of the 12 Houses or Bhavas, 
whatever be the sign and whatever be the degree ‘position) in 
that sign. The ascendant is the horizontal radius praceediaz 
from the centre leftwards, The Descendant or the 7th. cusp }s 
the ather horizontal line proceeding in the right from the Centre, 
The perpendicular one drawn vettically upwards, shows the 
point overhead i.¢., the meridian of the tocality of birth which is 
the Cusp of the 101b house. Diametrically, in the opposite 


direction, vertically dowo from the Centre, a line is Jrawo 
which is the 41h Cusp and it is termed Nadir. Houses 12, tI, 16 
(d)

zre the vistble hemisphere, the eastera hilf of the bonzon abyws 
the earth. The [2th Bhava extends from the Ascendant to the

12th Cusp. The IIth Bhava or House extends from the Ith 
Cusp to the Lith Cusp. The 10th House is spread over from Lith 
Cusp to the 10th and so on, The Lagna Bhava ?s to rise. So 
from the ascendant, counterclockwise below the earth, the 
Lagna Bhava extends upto the 2nd Cusp. The Second Bhava is 
between 2nd Cusp and the 3rd. The houses 9, 8 and 7 are above 
horizon and they occupy the visible western horizon. It extends 
from above the head to the point in the west where the earth 
appears ta touch the horizon—setting point called Descendant 
Houses f, 2, 3, 4, 5 and 6 are below horizon, 


While making out a horoscope, they use this map, and-insert 
planets in such relative positions as they appear in the Zodiac, 
The exact degree and mioute with the symbol of the sign ia 
which a Cusp falls is noted at the end of the radius oear the 
circumference. They use only the SAYANa PosiTion.

Vimshoddbari Dasa 


The Hindus take the exact position of Moon at the time of 
birth, fiuc) out 1a which constellation (Nakshatra, star Moon 
then was, the planet that governs tbe constellation, the total 
number of Solar year allotted to that planet, ihe position or the 
star already passed before birth and the balance of the Naksha- 
thra that ig to run: after calculation, find out the balance for the 
period that is to puss from the time of birth and then the order 
of the dasa is to be followed. 

They call the periods of the plancts as **Dasas**, The Hiodu 
sages bave adopted and advocated many dasas of which three are 
largely in use. Tocy are Vimshoddhari Dasa, Ashtottars Dasa and 
Kalachakra Dasa, Of these, the Yimsboddhari ts found to be 
very simple, most accurate and scientific. 

They have taken the seven planets (who rule the seven days

What is a Year! 


Some astrologers take a year as cqulvalent to 360 days while 
a few others take it as 765} days for purposes of calculation. 
Which is correct, and which is to be followed ? 


No explanation is available in any work of astrology as te 
how the sages had assigned 6 years to Sun, 10 to Moon, 7 tu Mars, 
and soon. But all have not definitely stated whether one is to 
take 3644 days or 360 days for purposes of calculation. 


So a doubt arises as Hlodus adopt five differeot Manams or 
methods for finding out the commencement of a year, its 
duration and its end, which syochronises with the beginniog of 
the succeeding year, They are as under: 


1. Soura Manam: This year commences ftom the time Sun 
enters the Nirayaua Mesha (Aries) and runs through until the 
Sun conipletes the thirtieth degree of Mecoa (Pisces), and is 
about to enter the sign Mesha again. This is araued 365} daya. 


2. Chandra Manam: This year commences from the Spvkla- 
paksha Prathamai Thithi of Chaithra month (Le., the first thithi 
after the New Moon Pangun! month according to Souramanam) 
extends over nearly 354 days and ends when the New Moon io 
Panguni month is over. 


97 


3. Savana Manam: A day la the Interval of time between 
successive occasions of Sun rise. Savana Manam year has 
exactly 360 such days. 


4. Nakshathra Manam: The interval of time between 
successive occasions of Moon's entry into Aswini Nakshatkra, Is 
called Nakshathra Mana mouth. Twelve such months make one 
Nakshathra Mana year. This works out to nearly 34 days, 


5. Bharhasapathyam: This year represents the duration 
of time taken by Jupiter (Guru of Brihaspathi) to transit in a 
sign This, on an average, works out to 361 days. 


Thus, there are five different years, and each is followed for 
different purposes. 


For calculating the Udu Dasa system, one has to follow only 
Soura ManaM. Mantreswarar, in Paladheepika, has meationed 
that one completes a year only when the Suo again enters 
inta the same longitude, as if was at the time of the birth 
of the native, That is, Mantreswarar takes a year, as the inter- 
val of time taken by Sun to leave aay one position in the Zodiac 
and again reach the same position. In other words, the year is 
reckoned as the time taken by the Sun to revolve exactly once in 
its apparent path, the ecliptict Mantreswarar says : 


qiend asnat aged, aafaus afaaqae | 
ama: afea queqrat wionarafgarea: seta: UI 


and is of firm opinion that Sovra Manam system alone is to be 
followed for Udu dasa Phala Nirnayam. 


Various commentators and authors of books have explained 
that Soura Manam is sued for the calculations of Pinda Ayurd- 
haya Dasa, Ayutdhaya Vibhaga, Prayaschitha, Sathra (Yagam) 
éte: Hence, only Soura Manam, i.e., year made up of 365} days 
alone is to be adopted. 


For convenience and for easy calculation of sub sub periods 
360 days are taken, as the difference is negligible. But 365} days 
must be taken,

Suppose a child is born at 3 a.m. after Sunset on 
31+12-1965 and before the Sun rise on Happy New Year day on 
1-1-1966 at a Latitude of 30° and Longitude 80°. It is found 
that the Sun rises at 6-56 a.m. Local Time. As the locality is 
&O0* East then the G.M.T. is 6-56 a.m. (80x4’) = 6-56 minus 
5-20= 1-36 a.m, 

As Indian Standard Time is always 54 hours in advance to 
G.M.T., the Indian Standard time will be 5-30+1.36 (G.M.T.)= 
7 hours 6 mts. a.m. 


Therefore Sun rises at 6-46 a.m. L.M.T. on 31-12-65 at 
80°F and 30°N. It means that the Sun rises at 7-06 a.m. LS.T. 
on 31-12-65 at 80°E. 


So the Interyal between the time of birth fe. 3 a.m, 1.8.T, 
and the time of Sun rise whicb is 7-00 am. [.$‘T.=1) hra. 
54 mts,—45 Ghatis (19 x 24 Ghatis+ 54x 2} Vikatis'. 

The astrologer will usually write 49 Ghatis 45 Vikatis after 
Suo rise on Friday 31-12-65. 


Actually date of birth is I-1-1966 as the midnight has passed 
and the New Year is born. 

Some astrologers write Friday and also 1-1-66. Soch confu- 
sions do arise in many cases. Hence the students should follow 
that method which is clear. So one fs to write that a person is 
born at 3 a.m. en 31-12-65/.-!-66 on Friday/Saturday. Theo 
there will be no doubt.

ASPECTS AND THEIR SIGNIFICANCE 


While examining a horoscope, various elements have to be 
considered, analysed in detail and combiaoed together with logical 
judgment. Once the fundamentals, namely the characteristics of 
the planets, the aatuce of the twelve zodiacal signs, the matters 
governed by the different Houses (Bhavas), the quality of the 
aspects etc., have been well comprehended, then the proper 
analysis and application will assist the astrologer to assess the 
tesults accurately. Practice makea one preheot to apply 
properly such roles and dicta. 


Each house by itself sigoifies certain affairs in one chart, A 
planet olaced In a house modifies and activates the mattera 
embraced by the house :—according to the planet’s inherent 
nature, Its Adhipathyam (ownership of houses in the horoscope) 
and its occupation. The sign and the star are the containers. 
The planet js the content. Milk in red glass tumbler will appear 
ted like blood. But ita colour changes due to that of the con- 
tainer. Similarly, the results of the planets will get modified or

WHAT 18 AN ASPECT ? 


An aspect Is ‘‘an angle formed on the Barth by the beams of 
two planets” (Kepler). The term is employed, in Astrology, to 
refer to angular distance measured in degree and minute of celes- 
tial longitude between two points lo the zodiac, According to 
Westerners, aspects are formed between two planets, or between 
a plaact and a point inthe horoscope, as for instance the cusp 
of a house (bhava), aay Ascendant M.C., etc. ‘Cusp’ may be 
defined as the edge of a house, i.e., the exact beginniog of a house 
(which is also the ead of the previous house) rekoned In degree 
and minute of celestial longitude. It ts established that some 
angular distances do produce resulta which are effective, 


A planet is considered to be in aspect with another planet of 
a sensitive point in the zodiac only when it is from the plauct on 
the sensitive point by a certaln longitudinal distance. 


110 


Planets are ever moving in their orbits in the zodiac and so 
aspects may be formed between them and the difference in their 
longitudes will form 4 particular aspect. That is, at some time, 
in some part of the zodiac, two planets may be transiting at a 
particular distance from each other and certain angles are formed 
upon the cacth by such relationship. [t has been found that 
these angles exert some influence on those individuals, anlmates 
or inanimates who are receptive to such vibrations by reason 
of the fact that, at their birth, vibrations of like nature were in 
operation.

APPLYING AND SEPARATING: When two heavenly bodies ate 
within the range of conjunction or aspect aod not in exact as rant

the influence reaches iis peak or maximum, but as soon as the 
quicker planet begins to move away from tbe culminating point, 
the influence still remains bot decreases slowly. When the 
quicker planet goes fully beyond the orb of conjunction, the 
influenc? completely ceases. Suppose one observes two gentis- 
man riding along a road, ouc on a moter cycle and the other ona 
bicycle. He who is on the motor cycle is at a certain distance

ata particular spot he will catch the latter (which is rapt coajun- 
ctlon), Thereafter, they continue theic joutney and slowly the 
distance between the t vo incressc« iwhich fs similar to separating 
asp cli and after, a certain time the man on the motor cycis i8 
not sighted. Likewise, the planets have different speeds. {nthe 
course of theirs travel in the sare direction the swifter planet, if 
hehind the slow-moving one, is said to be applying to the latter 
and at a particular point both are in exact conjunction-As soon 
as the conjunction or aspect is complete, the swifter planet will 
wove away from it when it is said to be separating from the 
conjunction or aspect as ihe case may be.

MUTUAL APPLIVAIION 


When two planets are within the range of an aspect and one 
of them is In retrograde motion i.c., moving against the natural 
order of the signs), cach will move towards the other. This is 
called ‘Mutual Application’. 


For example, Mars in ine Cancer 8 degrees and Jupiter in 
Vrischika !4 degrees. Mars is moving in direct motion ; that is, 
he is moving from Cancer 8 degress towards Cancer 14 degrees, 
Jupiter, on the ather hand, is retrograde, ie, he is retractlag 
from Vrischika «Scorpio} 14 degrces and is moving backwards. 
50 Mars is moving towards Jupiter and Jupiter is moving towarda 
Mars. Dee to the combined motions of these two planets, the 
trine is in the process of being formed very quickly. This is 
termed ‘Mutual Application’.

THE VARIOUS ASPECTS IN A NATAL CHART MAY BE PREPARED IN A

Aspect Is very important to study the results of a horoscope.

nf all the planets. Suppose Moon is In 10° Mesha (Aries). Ita 
planet were to be in Mesha between 10° and 30°, count the exact 
oumber of degrees at which the planet is away from the Moon.

Aries. Similarly, one should find out the plauets in Mitnuna 
(Gemini, Kataka (Cancer) andiso oa and calculate the relative 
position, Whether there is apy aspect or oot, one has to note

relween the different planets, (2} between the phinets and the

PRINCIPLES OF JUDGMBNT 


Aspects are the modifying influences in hotoscope and a

if platic, aote whether the aspect is in the process of

CONVERSION OF THE INDIAN STANDARD Time INTO Local MEAN

The Earth moves in an ellipse round the Sun and revolvey 
once on its axis in 24 hours. It takes 4 minutes to revolve one 
degree from west to east direction, and thereby every solar body 
appears to move from east to west Hence in places io the same 
latitude about one degree esst of any place the Sun rise will be 
seen 4 mts. earlier than places in the west. India Standard 
Time is always § bra. 30 mts. abead of Greenwich Meso Time 
because it is fixed for the longitude 82 deg. 30 mts. EB. For the
